7.1    Acoustic exclusion at the exact matching call site
Lemma 7.5 (No massless retained reference at matching). In the exact post-compact joint partition of
Corollary 6.40, the retained compact field has product normalized Haar reference and has already been
integrated coefficientwise before the optical connected logarithm is taken. The massless tangent Feynman
Gaussian used in the weak normal-chart construction is not a live matching reference. The only Gaussian
reference left live is the normalized optical one-core product, and every cross-core optical term is contained
in the explicit inventory AJG .
Proof. The exact compact collect of Theorem 6.39 and the post-compact identity of Corollary 6.40 restore
product Haar on the retained compact variables and place every nonempty retained dependence into the
compact, Gaussian, regular, or irregular activity inventories. The weak tangent Gaussian contributes
no undeclared covariance edge to this matching logarithm. By contrast, the optical one-core references
remain live, while their off-diagonal quadratic blocks and other cross-core terms occur explicitly in AJG
and retain the spatial payment used in Theorem 6.43.
Lemma 7.6 (Vacuum factorization after the complete owner map). Fix the optical variables and expand
one compact coefficient in Peter–Weyl sectors. Suppose every compact cut separating two declared root
buffers carries the trivial representation. Remove only the cross-core objects already registered in the
four disjoint inventories of Corollary 6.40. If no registered connector remains between the roots, the
residual coefficient factorizes into one-core expectations and empty-support scalars. Its contribution to the
normalized connected two-root logarithm is zero.
Proof. Triviality on every separating compact cut permits repeated Haar–Fubini separation. The exact
incidence-split collect retains every internally nontrivial singlet in AJH , while the owner map assigns every
other cross-core occurrence to exactly one of AJG , AJreg , AJirr , or AJH . After those registered objects are
removed, the residual history has no object joining distinct one-core components. It therefore factorizes.
Empty-support scalars are removed by the normalization, and products of separate one-root expectations
are removed by the connected logarithm.
Theorem 7.7 (Exact acoustic exclusion-or-capture). Consider any connected zero-, one-, or two-source
coefficient joining two bounded matching-scale root buffers in the exact joint logarithm. At least one object
on the unique root-to-root forest path belongs to one of the following classes:
   (i) a nonvacuum compact activity controlled by Theorem 6.39 and the spatial row (99);
  (ii) an optical covariance bond or an optical Gaussian cross-core activity in AJG ;
 (iii) a registered reference, regular, irregular, seam, reflection, wrapping, normalization, determinant, or
       source connector with its original support payment.
There is no fourth class consisting of a massless retained acoustic reference edge.
Proof. Assume that a connected coefficient contains no object of types (i)–(iii). Absence of (i) makes
every separating compact cut vacuum. Absence of (iii) leaves no registered physical connector after the
complete owner map. Lemma 7.6 then reduces the compact contribution to disconnected one-core factors
and scalars. Absence of (ii) also prevents the still-live optical field from joining the roots. The coefficient
is therefore disconnected, a contradiction. The only possible remaining concern would be an undeclared
massless retained covariance, which is excluded by Lemma 7.5.

Corollary 7.8 (No additional acoustic path class). The exact vertex inventory used in Theorem 6.43 ad-
mits no fourth root-to-root path class consisting of a massless retained acoustic reference edge. Consequently
the boundary-to-center estimates (104) and (107) require no additional terminal Witten, acoustic-channel,
or retained-Gaussian gap hypothesis.
Proof. The vertex inventory is already exhaustive by Lemma 6.7, Proposition 6.8, and Corollary 6.40.
Apply Theorem 7.7 to exclude the only remaining acoustic fourth-class concern.

                                                     74
Exact finite-depth multiscale forest formula For a microscopic depth m, let Rj denote the exact
normalized conditional-logarithm map that integrates the scale-j fluctuation field and returns the source-
decorated effective interaction at scale j − 1. The maps include the regular/irregular partition of unity
before the logarithm is taken, so no all-good denominator occurs. Write

                                     Wm (J) = log Zm (J) − log Zm (0)

for the normalized finite-volume source logarithm. Iterating the exact maps Rm , . . . , Rjw and then the
finite macrobridge is an identity, not an asymptotic expansion.
   A scale-labelled source forest is generated recursively as follows. A source derivative is a labelled leaf.
At one conditional integration, connected cumulant differentiation joins a nonempty partition of the
incoming leaves and decorated subtrees to one new vertex labelled by that integration scale. Terms
transported without joining retain their existing components. At the terminal matching logarithm, the
remaining components are joined by the matching-scale connected cumulants. The ordering of children is
fixed by the least source label, so isomorphic terms are not counted more than once.
Lemma 8.6 (Exact multiscale Faà di Bruno forest formula). For every finite depth m and every fixed
derivative order n,                                        X
                             Dn Wm (0)[J1 , . . . , Jn ] =   Am (τ ; J1 , . . . , Jn ),                 (115)
                                                      τ ∈Tm,n

where Tm,n is the finite family of recursively generated scale-labelled connected trees described above. Each
analytic coefficient of each exact conditional logarithm occurs exactly once. After absolute values are
taken, the sum is dominated uniformly in m by the rooted weak rows and the finite matching expansion.
Hence (115) passes to the infinite weak-depth limit termwise for every fixed n.
Proof. For one conditional integration, differentiation of the normalized logarithm is the joint-cumulant
identity. The set-partition formula for a derivative of a composition is precisely the Banach-valued Faà di
Bruno formula: every block of the partition is one incoming connected component and the outer cumulant
joins those blocks at the current scale. Fixing the least-label order makes this representation unique.
Induction over the finite sequence of exact conditional integrations proves (115); the terminal matching
logarithm is the final induction step. The weak rooted margin pays every nonroot weak vertex by σ < 1,
while the finite macrobridge and matching KP expansion pay the finitely many terminal vertices. The
one/two-root bounds control a component while its source leaves remain external. These majorants are
summable independently of m, so dominated convergence gives the last assertion.

    For a tree carrying two distinguished source buffers, let v(A, B) be their unique lowest common ancestor.
If v(A, B) belongs to the terminal matching logarithm, call the history a matching history. Otherwise its
scale label j(v) is the unique weak coarsest-connection scale. Notice that this is a property of the exact
recursively generated tree, not a choice made after absolute values or bounds have been applied.
Corollary 8.7 (No omission and no double counting). The connected two-buffer part of D2 Wm (0) is
the disjoint sum of the matching histories and the weak histories indexed by their unique label j ≥ jw .
Contracting all strict descendants of v(A, B) produces a one/two-root decorated vertex whose norm is
bounded uniformly by Theorem 7.2 and Corollary 7.4. The decomposition remains exact after regular and
irregular sectors are recombined. Every connected tree has exactly one lowest common ancestor for the two
source leaves, and the recursive least-label convention gives it exactly one representation in Lemma 8.6.
Descendants have strictly finer labels or belong to the same finite conditional vertex and are summed by
its connected cumulant. Exact regular/irregular recombination precedes the conditional logarithm at every
scale, so the forest formula expands the full measure rather than a conditioned all-good measure.

Unique-coarsest-scale allocation Differentiate the exact composition of normalized conditional
logarithms with respect to source parameters. Every connected term has a finite collection of local objects
carrying scale labels. Contract every maximal connected collection of finer-scale objects into a decorated
root and declare the largest physical scale remaining to be the coarsest scale of the history. Ties are
broken lexicographically.
Lemma 8.8 (Canonical multiscale allocation). Every connected two-buffer source history is assigned
exactly once either to the matching connector or to one weak scale j ≥ jw . Finer decorations attached to
its coarsest skeleton have a uniformly summable one/two-root norm. Put

                                             dw := CR + m−1
                                                         w .

                               j≥jw

For d < dw the complete history is retained in one fixed enlarged local cluster: it is uniformly bounded for
bounded observables and controlled in the local source topology for renormalized fields.
Proof. By Corollary 8.7, the lowest common ancestor of the two source buffers assigns every connected
tree to exactly one terminal or weak-scale class. This is the first scale in the exact recursive conditional-
logarithm formula at which the two source components are joined. The least-label convention removes
automorphism multiplicities before estimates are applied. The fixed root algebra sums every finer
decoration independently of the number of activePnonroot supports. Since ℓj = 2−(j−jw ) ℓjw , put

and then this is at most C1 exp(−x), proving (116). If d < dw the two buffers lie in one fixed enlarged
physical neighborhood. Their ultraviolet contact subtrees are not summed as separate long connectors;
they are contracted into the exact local atom described in the statement.

Proof. Combine Lemma 8.5 with the exact forest allocation and the weak scale sum. For d ≥ dloc choose

degraded by increasing the ultraviolet depth.
   For bounded observables, the covariance and every short-distance connected local atom are bounded
by their sup norms, so enlarging A0 extends the bound to all separations. Lemma 8.1 and integrability of
the physical exponential kernel give (118). For a normalized local source, the short-distance cluster is
not estimated pointwise. It is exactly the local source coefficient renormalized by the finite triangular
counterterm matrix; the finite triangular source theorem printed in Part V bounds it in a finite Schwartz
seminorm. Once two source supports are separated, the lowest-common-ancestor edge lies outside both
local clusters and the same connector exponent applies. Thus the theorem derives a cutoff-independent
physical rate without suppressing or hiding contact terms.

Corollary 8.12 (Uniform source analyticity and exponential clustering). On a fixed neighborhood of the
origin, every finite source functional is analytic uniformly in resolution and volume. Thermodynamic
and zero-spacing limits of all fixed finite source derivatives exist along one common diagonal subsequence.
If two groups of source supports are separated by physical distance d, their connected full moments are
bounded by Ce−md with the same m > 0 as in Theorem 8.9. The local source incidence and Lemma 8.3,
together with the finite compact matching expansion, give a common analytic source ball. Theorem 8.11
permits termwise differentiation and a countable diagonal extraction. The finite-volume approximants are
chosen on a cofinal schedule from Corollary 6.2. The extraction is made once for the countable algebra
and a fixed countable set of dyadic physical source scales, and the resulting subsequence is relabelled as
the directed sequence appearing in Theorem 1.1; no uniqueness or schedule-independence claim is needed.
In every connected partition joining two separated groups, at least one tree edge crosses the separation;
factor its exponential and sum all remaining edges with (118). Full moments follow from the set-partition
formula.

Lemma 8.14 (Reflection positivity inheritance under exact recombination). Every limiting positive-time
quadratic form used in the common Schwinger system is the limit of a reflection-positive quadratic form
under the original Wilson measure. Exact normal-chart, coarea, compact-pivot, and source transfor-

Theorem 8.15 (Common positive-scale Schwinger system). The common subsequence defines tempered,
permutation-symmetric, reflection-positive, continuously translation-covariant Schwinger distributions for
the countable algebra generated by the bounded positive-scale observables and every fixed finite normalized
local source family. The distributions are projectively compatible and satisfy uniform physical exponential
clustering.
Proof. The Schwartz estimate gives equicontinuity on each finite tensor power. Diagonal extraction over
a countable dense test/source family gives the common projective system. Permutation symmetry passes
as an algebraic identity, while Lemma 8.14 gives the complete reflection-positivity mechanism after exact
label recombination. Riemann- sum translation errors vanish by the physical Schur bound, Corollary 8.12
gives clustering, and Lemma 8.13 gives the OS linear-growth row.

8.2    Ward identities and Euclidean covariance
Theorem 8.16 (Fixed-group invariant-jet admission through dimension six). For a compact simple Lie
algebra g:
(I1) the invariant symmetric bilinear forms form a one-dimensional space;
(I2) there is no reflection-compatible gauge-invariant local vector of engineering dimension at most four;
(I3) modulo total differences, equations of motion, and the reflection-odd topological density, the reflection-
     even scalar dimension-four block is generated by the Yang–Mills density;
(I4) the gauge-invariant jet quotient through dimension six is finite. A primitive cubic symmetric color
     tensor occurs precisely for Lie type Ar with r ≥ 2 and is retained in the dimension-six block; its
      presence does not enlarge the dimension-four marginal block.
The filtered finite-matrix transport and associated-graded contraction used below therefore apply for every
fixed G after enlarging only the finite jet dimension.
Proof. Simplicity gives the uniqueness of the invariant bilinear form. Semisimplicity makes every finite-
dimensional representation traceless on g. Lorentz index counting, gauge covariance, and reflection parity
then exclude a single free vector index through dimension four and leave only the quadratic curvature
scalar in the even dimension-four sector. Chevalley’s theorem makes the invariant polynomial algebra
finitely generated; the degrees are the exponents plus one. Through degree three a primitive symmetric
tensor exists only for type Ar , and it first multiplies three curvatures, hence has engineering dimension
six. At every fixed engineering cutoff only finitely many covariant derivatives and invariant contractions
occur.

Lemma A.3 (Exact even-contact division of complete packets). For every complete packet class λ,
the one-open-leg coefficient vanishes by global color invariance. The renormalized two-open-leg packet is
reflection even, vanishes at p = 0, and admits the exact second-order division
                                                      X
                                    Tλ (p) − Tλ (0) =    pbα pbβ Sλ,αβ (p).                        (188)
                                                       α,β

For every 0 < a′ < a0 , the rooted norm of Sλ is bounded by the second support moment of the same
complete packet at exponent a0 .
   If the complete packet is represented at weak scale i, the two displayed difference factors carry the
canonical relative factor
                                             (r/ℓi )2 = 2−2i                                       (189)
with respect to the fixed physical source scale r. This factor belongs to the whole packet. It is not assigned
to a chosen branch or attachment cut.
Proof. The selector is inserted only after the exact normalized packet is formed and is scalar under color,
translation and aligned reflection. Hence every selected sum is again a complete packet. Its one-open-leg
coefficient is an invariant adjoint vector and therefore vanishes. Global contact subtraction is allocated
after the disjoint packet decomposition, so each selected renormalized coefficient vanishes at p = 0.
Reflection gives Tλ (−p) = Tλ (p) and therefore removes the linear Taylor term. The integral Taylor
formula, or equivalently finite Laurent telescoping in the variables eipα , gives (188). Two momentum
derivatives insert two support displacements, absorbed by the unused exponent a0 − a′ .
   The divided packet has no reflection-even scalar or rank-two local component of engineering dimension
four or five. Lemmas 5.22 and 5.23 therefore place its local projection in the stable degree-six block; this
observation will be used only for stable membership, not to estimate the nonlocal packet.
   The scale factor in (189) is instead purely geometric. In the tangent-normalized profile coordinates, one
lattice difference at representation scale ℓi = 2i r is (r/ℓi ) times the corresponding unit-scale difference.
The two explicit factors in (188) therefore contribute exactly (r/ℓi )2 = 2−2i . The two support moments
of Sλ are absorbed by the unused rooted exponent. This argument applies to the whole packet, local and
nonlocal together. Since the division identity holds for the entire C 2 family, zero, one or two g derivatives
do not remove the two explicit differences.


                                                       95
Lemma A.4 (g-marked one-scale admission). There is g0 > 0 such that, for real 0 ≤ g ≤ g0 , the complete
normalized interaction and all declared source/defect marks are twice continuously differentiable in the
permanent regulator space. An undistinguished non-Gaussian child has parent-normalized row

                                                 η0 (g) ≤ Cg,

while a child carrying one or two distinguished g derivatives has row

                                              η1 (g) + η2 (g) ≤ C.

The exactly recombined large-field/irregular row and its first two g derivatives extend continuously by zero
                                             2
at g = 0 and are bounded by Cm g −pm e−c/g for m ≤ 2. No claim that an attached odd child is O(g 2 ) is
made.
Proof. In the scale-normalized Gaussian coordinate, the cubic Yang–Mills species starts at order g, the
quartic and other non-Gaussian even species start at order g 2 , and the common species tube controls
all field and source marks. Thus an unmarked species row is Cg, while one or two explicit g derivatives
remove at most the corresponding powers and give a finite row. The regulator allocations are additive
under multiplication, and the Gram and Pruefer estimates in the summed replica–BKAR theorem are
insensitive to the parity of an attached tensor. The ordinary parent-normalized child recursion therefore
closes for Cg0 below its printed threshold, with two fixed derivative marks handled by Leibniz. The
C 3 -flat irregular estimate in u = g 2 gives the displayed first and second g-derivative rows after the chain
rule. In particular the model child gA3 differentiated once by a covariance edge is correctly admitted as
an O(g) child, and one g derivative of that child is O(1).
Lemma A.5 (Cellwise complete shell decomposition). Fix the finite directed depth M and one crossing
cell, including the no-crossing cell. After the selected exact factorization has been completed, apply the
canonical reported-support selector only to whole normalized packets. The separated part of the two-root
functional has an exact disjoint decomposition
                                                       X

whose coefficients are C 2 on the relative interior of that cell. Every normalization, denominator, localiza-

Proof. At fixed finite volume and depth, the reporting-telescope and owner-map identities form a disjoint
finite or absolutely convergent inventory of complete normalized packets. Their canonical physical reported
supports therefore admit disjoint scalar dyadic selectors after packet completion. Inside one crossing

finite marked forest permit termwise differentiation. Summing the selectors gives the unselected exact
functional, proving (190).

extraction identity therefore give EX Ωi,X = 0 for every reported support X; all three off-graph coordinates
lie in the actual centered stable fiber. The first coordinate is the canonical sum of histories carrying a
wrapping certificate. After the tangent-normalized scalar coordinate is extracted, the massless acoustic
sum–integral correction belongs to the nonautonomous scalar map Fi and is not an off-graph activity.
By the exact regular interaction comparison and label recombination, the only nonwrapping off-graph
difference is the slaved U4 charged/atlas complement; this is Bi and is exponentially flat in ui . No flat
charged coefficient is identified with a wrapping history.

Proof. Use the complete labelled connected-history representation before forgetting owners. In the
scheduled torus and in the rooted thermodynamic step, pair a history whenever its canonically reported
hull has an injective periodic lift and contains no charged atlas label. The local Karcher, Gaussian, coarea,
determinant, localization, extraction and collect templates are then literally the same, so paired histories
and their derivatives cancel coefficientwise. An unpaired regular history has a nontrivial periodic cycle;
its reported hull has length at least cMi . Weakening the permanent rooted support exponent once gives
Ce−σ0 Mi . An unpaired charged history belongs to the exactly recombined U4 row and is Cu−P e−c0 /u .
   For (193), insert H as one distinguished input atom before the connected logarithm. The permanent
one-step derivative envelope is a rooted absolute sum with that atom retained once. The same periodic-lift
pairing applies, and the long hull still pays e−σ0 Mi ; no derivative can shorten the already assigned reported
hull. Vacuum, two-jet and coupling extraction are finite-range linear maps and are paired in the same way.


                                                       97
Passing to the adapted norm is harmless: that norm is a fixed finite sum of rooted norms of powers of the
rooted-thermodynamic linear map, and each power preserves the preceding paired/unpaired classification.
   At R = R⋆ (u), the free term cancels because both maps are written in the same schedule-fixed vacuum
coordinates. The stable tangent uH1 is exponentially local but need not have finite support, so an
unpaired periodic image may already be of order εi u. This sharp row gives (192); no quadratic stable
cancellation is asserted. If the output is projected to the wrapping ideal, a charged history without a
prior periodic certificate must also acquire a new nontrivial periodic cycle. It therefore pays both the
charged flat row and εi+1 . The same observation applied to a nonwrapping distinguished input proves
(194) and (195). These are statements about the full centered interaction map, not inferences from the
scalar comparison.
   Finally differentiate the labelled identities before summing histories. A g mark either stays on the
distinguished input or hits one of finitely many local atoms, the scalar orbit, the extraction projector,
or a new periodic connector. In the first case the same pairing proves the same-order coefficient in the
statement. In every other case the marked child row and the trajectory ledger give the polynomial factor,
while the periodic hull or charged event remains present. This proves (196) through two marks.

Lemma A.11 (Exact finite-depth twice-marked conditional forest). At finite directed depth M , differen-
tiate the exact factorization of Definition A.6 with respect to the two curvature roots and at most two
copies of g. The result is a finite sum of complete scale-labelled packet trees such that:
    (i) the two source leaves have one unique lowest common ancestor;
   (ii) each g mark is owned exactly once by a trajectory coordinate, a conditional logarithm, a source
        return, the terminal weak logarithm, or the compact handoff logarithm;
  (iii) every covariance edge, hard-core derivative, field factorial, regulator allocation and normalization
        contact belongs to one complete one-step packet before multiscale composition;
  (iv) the coefficients are exactly those of the Banach-valued Faà di Bruno formula.
No infinite-depth passage is asserted in this lemma.
Proof. Apply the joint-cumulant derivative identity to every exact normalized weak conditional and, on
a crossing cell, to the exact finite macrobridge and joint compact–optical logarithm. Then apply the
Banach-valued Faà di Bruno formula to the finite composition. This assertion is coefficientwise and uses
only finite-volume differentiability; the uniform compact marked bound is supplied later by Theorem A.20.
The one-step summed replica–BKAR theorem controls each weak packet, Lemma A.4 controls zero,
one and two g marks, and finite source transport controls the external roots. Uniqueness of the lowest
common ancestor is the ordinary tree property.
Lemma A.12 (Top marked ancestor and complete-packet shell). In a packet tree from Lemma A.11,
contract every maximal descendant subtree carrying no distinguished label into its exact parent-normalized
decoration, and suppress every maximal unary chain into its exact ordered transfer product. The distin-
guished labels are the two curvature roots and the zero, one or two active g marks. They have a unique
lowest common ancestor v⊤ ; let j be its conditional scale. Call v⊤ the top marked ancestor.
    All vertices strictly above v⊤ are unmarked. The complete coefficient created at v⊤ includes every
contracted side decoration with its canonical reported support. Decompose these whole coefficients by their
full reported-support shell k using a scalar translation- and reflection-invariant selector. The selected
class is therefore a complete BRS/reflection packet with every denominator and normalization contact
still inside it.
    If j ≤ k + C0 , the permanent rooted-support row of that whole packet gives
                                                                 k−j
                                            C(1 + 2k−j )q e−c2         .                                   (208)

If j > k + C0 , the full support lies inside a fixed enlargement of one scale-j cell and no spatial connector
estimate is needed.

Proof. Contraction of an unlabelled descendant is its exact conditional value, so it retains its canonical
reported support. Suppression of a unary chain is the exact ordered transfer product. After these
operations, a tree with at most four distinguished leaves has only a fixed number of essential branching
edges, while all arbitrarily long unary portions are already contained in the parity transfer product. The
unique lowest common ancestor of all labels is an ordinary tree invariant. If a g derivative lands on a
coarser conditional map, its mark is attached at that map and therefore moves v⊤ to that scale. Hence
no marked vertex can occur above v⊤ .
   The shell selector is applied only after the full packet at v⊤ has been collected. A remote unmarked
side decoration is not cut from its parent and is not asked to carry an external Taylor factor. If exact
localization turns its contribution into a smaller-hull contact, that term is already in the finite contact block
and is not a shell-k packet. Otherwise its canonical reported support remains part of the whole packet.
When that support spans 2k−j scale-j blocks, the strict rooted-support norm and the weak connector
theorem give (208), including covariance, hard-core and mixed alternatives. The exact Loc/collect identity
of Theorem 5.8 ensures that this dichotomy is made before the stable packet is declared.

C     The fixed-group Sobolev and Peter–Weyl ledger
Let λπ be the Laplace eigenvalue of an irreducible representation π and dπ its dimension. By Peter–Weyl
and the spectral Weyl law [16],
                                  X                                                dG

Therefore sG = ⌊dG /4⌋ + 1 gives 2sG > dG /2 and

                                      ∥f ∥A(G) ≤ CG,sG ∥(1 − ∆G )sG f ∥2 .
                                                                     |I|
For a pivot product GI , tensorization gives the constant CG,sG . The original-support reserve absorbs its
logarithm after the finite matching separation is enlarged.

                       Group               dim G      sG        coefficient 11CA log 2/(24π 2 )
                       SU(4)                 15       4                    11 log 2/(6π 2 )
                       Sp(2) (USp(4))        10       3                    11 log 2/(8π 2 )
                       G2                    14       4                    11 log 2/(6π 2 )

   The higher Sobolev order concerns internal compact-pivot derivatives. It does not increase the physical
source-mark budget; those marks remain the fixed orders consumed by the boundary-to-center and Ward
arguments. In the ordered-multijet realization of Lemma A.17, one Sobolev monomial uses at most
eight distinct flow variables at an SU(4) or G2 pivot and at most six at an Sp(2) pivot. Repeated color
directions retain distinct variables. Thus, for example, D12 D22 is represented by the ordered word (1, 1, 2, 2)
rather than by a fourth derivative along a single flow.

D      Ordinary periodic torons, seams, and wrapping templates
The finite-volume model used throughout is the ordinary periodic product space GE(Λ) with periodic
gauge transformations. It is a single, explicit lattice model; no twisted boundary condition and no sum
over nontrivial principal-bundle sectors is part of the construction. Since the target spacetime R4 is
contractible, this restriction is compatible with the theorem’s local continuum scope.
  Define the compact set of ordinary periodic flat holonomies

                          TG := {(h1 , h2 , h3 , h4 ) ∈ G4 : [hµ , hν ] = e for all µ, ν}.

Lemma D.1 (Tree-gauge reduction of ordinary periodic flat fields). Every flat configuration in the
ordinary periodic link model is gauge equivalent to a seam representative whose interior links are the
identity and whose four fundamental seam holonomies form an element of TG . Conversely every element
of TG defines such a flat periodic configuration. The remaining gauge action is simultaneous conjugation
of the four holonomies.


                                                          110
Proof. Choose a maximal lattice tree in a fundamental periodic cell and gauge its links to the identity.
Flatness makes the holonomy of a path depend only on its homotopy class. The four links closing the
fundamental cycles therefore supply holonomies hµ . The commutator loop of two fundamental cycles
is a product of plaquette boundaries, so flatness gives [hµ , hν ] = e. Reversing this construction gives
a flat seam representative from every commuting four-tuple. A gauge transformation at the tree root
conjugates all four holonomies simultaneously, and all other gauge freedom has already been fixed.
Lemma D.2 (Finite toron and seam atlas). For every fixed compact G, the set TG has a finite cover by
ambient product-coordinate neighborhoods on which every block word, Karcher word, coarea factor, Haar
factor, reflected word, and source word used in the proof is analytic in the local variables and in the toron
parameters. All derivatives through order 2sG + 3 have a finite common bound. The aligned coordinate
reflections and seam placements add only a finite template family.
Proof. The commutator equations define a closed subset of compact G4 , hence TG is compact. Around
each of its points choose ordinary product charts in the ambient manifold G4 ; no quotient chart or
classification of singular conjugacy classes is required. In tree gauge, every local factor is a fixed finite
composition of multiplication, inversion, the local exponential/logarithm, and the Karcher implicit
function, with some arguments left- or right-multiplied by the fixed seam holonomies. These maps are
analytic on a sufficiently small product neighborhood and have finite Cauchy bounds through the declared
order. Compactness gives a finite subcover. There are four seam directions and a finite aligned-reflection
family, so taking a finite maximum gives the common constants.
Theorem D.3 (Ordinary-periodic toron and optical admission). The complete full-alias right-inverse,
normal-section, optical-coercivity, Haar-minorization, owner, and boundary-to-center estimates hold
uniformly over every flat toron background in TG , every admitted seam and aligned-reflection template,
and every periodic wrapping certificate. The acoustic toron/background variables remain explicit and are
never inverted. No constant uniform over different groups is asserted.
Proof. Lift a seam representative to the universal covering lattice. A flat background is gauged to the
identity there; deck translation in direction µ acts on Lie-algebra fields by the unitary operator Ad(hµ ).
Since the hµ commute, these unitary operators are simultaneously diagonalizable over the complexified
color space. On each joint color eigenspace the folded block and optical symbols are therefore the
identity-background, color-free symbols evaluated at a real quasi-momentum shifted by the corresponding
deck phase. The estimates
                                                  49                      8
                                Af (k)Af (k)∗ ⪰      I4 ,   ∥P0 (k)∥2 ≤      ,
                                                   2                      49
and the printed complex-strip and optical lower bounds hold for every real quasi-momentum. They
consequently survive every toron phase without a loss of constant. The retained acoustic variables,
including toron tangents, are kept in the exterior/background register; only the same normal optical
complement is inverted.
   Lemma D.2 supplies the nonlinear, coarea, Haar, and marked derivative bounds uniformly over a finite
toron/seam cover. On the covering lattice the local gauge trivialization sends every plaquette holonomy
to a conjugate of the corresponding fluctuation plaquette. The representation-normalized Wilson function
is conjugation invariant, so the charge and its differentiated finite-word ledger are unchanged by this
trivialization. The 2sG differentiated bad-core row of Theorem 3.20 is therefore uniform on the same
cover. Consequently the complete one-pivot score and Haar minorization retain their printed constants
after taking the finite template maximum. On intersections use the fixed least-label owner, so no factor is
duplicated. A wrapping certificate records only the four lattice directions, the fixed block range, a finite
seam crossing pattern, and an aligned reflection type; there are finitely many such local templates at one
normalized step. Their iteration is already controlled by the permanent wrapping ideal rather than by a
sum over topological sectors. Taking the finite maximum proves the stated admission and leaves the joint
Haar–optical boundary-to-center mechanism unchanged.

E     Invariant tensor spaces through engineering dimension six
The low-dimensional color input can be stated without choosing a basis. The degree-two invariant
polynomial is unique for every simple g. A primitive degree-three invariant occurs only for Ar , r ≥ 2.
Hence the reflection-even dimension-four scalar block is one-dimensional for every G, while the dimension-
six block may contain the additional cubic curvature contraction in type A. The antisymmetric structure


                                                      111
tensor is always present through the bracket but contributes only inside the already retained covariant
derivative and commutator jets.
   The manuscript uses the complete finite quotient generated by curvature, covariant derivatives, metric
and epsilon contractions, invariant color tensors, total-difference classes, and equation-of-motion contacts
through the declared engineering cutoff. No argument relies on a hand-picked SU(2) basis once this
quotient is used.

statements that consume it. In particular, the completion proof in Theorem 11.1 does not rely on an
unprinted constant or on a rank-one identity.

   The table also separates the common mechanisms into four logical classes. Replica–BKAR, Kotecký–
Preiss, history compatibility, support reporting, least-label source forests, OS reconstruction, and spectral-
gap promotion are color-free. The full-alias symbol, normal right inverse, optical split, and spacetime

estimates enter only through the finite constants displayed above. Invariant jets and dimension-six color
tensors are enlarged finite spaces rather than rank-one identities.
   The rank-one paper [14] is obtained by taking G = SU(2), sG = 1, and a trivial cubic invariant space.
This map is comparative; the present proof does not invoke the rank-one theorem.

This appendix instantiates the finite ledgers for three non-rank-one groups. For a faithful representation
ρ, let Iρ > 0 be its Dynkin index in the declared invariant metric,

                                                     2dρ

SU(4).    Use the defining faithful representation on C4 :

                                                           1

                                      wG,ρ (U ) = cρ 1 − Re Tr U .

Then dG = 15, CA (G) = 4, sG = 4, and 2sG = 8 > 15/2. The degree-three invariant is present and is
included in the dimension-six jet block. The scale-two coefficient is 11 log 2/(6π 2 ). The charge vanishes
precisely at I4 and is positive away from it, so Theorem 3.4 applies with the representation-normalized
deficit rather than a rank-one raw trace.

Sp(2) (USp(4)).        Use the standard faithful four-dimensional complex representation:

                                                          1

G2 . Use the faithful seven-dimensional real fundamental representation, viewed unitarily after complexi-

                                                     1

Then dG = 14, CA (G) = 4, sG = 4, and 2sG = 8 > 7. There is no primitive cubic invariant and the
coefficient is 11 log 2/(6π 2 ). Faithfulness again makes the identity the unique zero of the charge.
   For all three examples, the compact ordinary-periodic toron set TG ⊂ G4 is covered by Lemma D.2;
Theorem D.3 then supplies the same color-free full-alias and optical constants. The spectral sum in
Appendix C converges at the displayed orders, while all nonlinear constants remain finite and group
dependent. These are concrete admissions, not hypotheses of the main theorem.

tensor is always present through the bracket but contributes only inside the already retained covariant
derivative and commutator jets.
   The manuscript uses the complete finite quotient generated by curvature, covariant derivatives, metric
and epsilon contractions, invariant color tensors, total-difference classes, and equation-of-motion contacts
through the declared engineering cutoff. No argument relies on a hand-picked SU(2) basis once this
quotient is used.

F      Theorem-level specialization and dependency map
The purpose of this appendix is provenance rather than a new estimate. It identifies every independent
fixed-group input in the register CG , the first proof call at which that dependence enters, and the later
statements that consume it. In particular, the completion proof in Theorem 11.1 does not rely on an
unprinted constant or on a rank-one identity.

   The table also separates the common mechanisms into four logical classes. Replica–BKAR, Kotecký–
Preiss, history compatibility, support reporting, least-label source forests, OS reconstruction, and spectral-
gap promotion are color-free. The full-alias symbol, normal right inverse, optical split, and spacetime
BPHZL degrees tensor with Ig . Karcher, action-charge, Peter–Weyl, and ordinary-periodic template
estimates enter only through the finite constants displayed above. Invariant jets and dimension-six color
tensors are enlarged finite spaces rather than rank-one identities.
   The rank-one paper [14] is obtained by taking G = SU(2), sG = 1, and a trivial cubic invariant space.
This map is comparative; the present proof does not invoke the rank-one theorem.

G      Representative fixed-group certifications
This appendix instantiates the finite ledgers for three non-rank-one groups. For a faithful representation
ρ, let Iρ > 0 be its Dynkin index in the declared invariant metric,
                                     − Tr dρ(X)dρ(Y ) = Iρ ⟨X, Y ⟩.
                                                        

The representation-normalized Wilson function has the expansion
                                                     cρ Iρ
                                      wG,ρ (eX ) =         ∥X∥2 + O(∥X∥4 ).
                                                     2dρ
The declared background-field convention fixes the positive scalar cρ ; any equivalent choice is related by
a finite linear change of the bare coupling.


                                                        113
SU(4).    Use the defining faithful representation on C4 :

                                                           1
                                                               
                                      wG,ρ (U ) = cρ 1 − Re Tr U .
                                                           4

Then dG = 15, CA (G) = 4, sG = 4, and 2sG = 8 > 15/2. The degree-three invariant is present and is
included in the dimension-six jet block. The scale-two coefficient is 11 log 2/(6π 2 ). The charge vanishes
precisely at I4 and is positive away from it, so Theorem 3.4 applies with the representation-normalized
deficit rather than a rank-one raw trace.

Sp(2) (USp(4)).        Use the standard faithful four-dimensional complex representation:

                                                          1
                                                                   
                                      wG,ρ (U ) = cρ 1 − Re Tr U .
                                                          4

Here dG = 10, CA (G) = 3, sG = 3, and 2sG = 6 > 5. There is no primitive cubic invariant, and the
coefficient is 11 log 2/(8π 2 ). The same faithful-action and differentiated charged-core ledger applies.

G2 . Use the faithful seven-dimensional real fundamental representation, viewed unitarily after complexi-
fication:
                                                     1
                                                                 
                                  wG,ρ (U ) = cρ 1 − Re Tr ρ(U ) .
                                                     7
Then dG = 14, CA (G) = 4, sG = 4, and 2sG = 8 > 7. There is no primitive cubic invariant and the
coefficient is 11 log 2/(6π 2 ). Faithfulness again makes the identity the unique zero of the charge.
   For all three examples, the compact ordinary-periodic toron set TG ⊂ G4 is covered by Lemma D.2;
Theorem D.3 then supplies the same color-free full-alias and optical constants. The spectral sum in
Appendix C converges at the displayed orders, while all nonlinear constants remain finite and group
dependent. These are concrete admissions, not hypotheses of the main theorem.
